## 1.2 MELD in outline

We <sub>p</sub>resent MELD (a name for the semantic meld, or mer<sub>g</sub>e, of knowled<sub>g</sub>e): a state-s<sub>y</sub>nchronization <sub>p</sub>rotocol for man<sub>y</sub> soverei<sub>g</sub>n a<sub>g</sub>ent wiki brains. Where MCP ex<sub>p</sub>oses tools and resources and a<sub>g</sub>entto-a<sub>g</sub>ent <sub>p</sub>rotocols ex<sub>p</sub>ose tasks [Goo<sub>g</sub>le and Linux Foundation 2025], MELD ex<sub>p</sub>oses meanin<sub>g</sub>s, claims<sub>,</sub> and mer<sub>g</sub>es. MELD is built around one o<sub>p</sub>eration that no tool <sub>p</sub>rotocol has: when a brain receives knowled<sub>g</sub>e from a <sub>p</sub>eer<sub>,</sub> it does not a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> that knowled<sub>g</sub>e directl<sub>y;</sub> it runs a mer<sub>g</sub>e decision <sub>proce</sub>d<sub>ure</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ncom</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t it <sub>a</sub>l<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>y</sub> h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>em</sub>it<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub> auditable record of the outcome (Figure 1): same-enough (the same fact, merged), overlaps (related but distinct, linked rather than collapsed), or conflicts (incompatible, the contradiction kept as a first-class object rather than silently resolved). A single operation, PUBLISH of a graph delta, carries th<sub>e</sub> k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>merge-on-rece</sub>i<sub>ve</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>cs</sub> <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>oa</sub>d<sub>-</sub>b<sub>ear</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>new</sub> id<sub>ea.</sub>

In autonomic-computing terms, this merge-on-receive loop makes MELD a self-managing coherence mechanism for a federation of agent memories, and the adapted run-time model is the knowled<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h itself. Each brain runs a <sub>p</sub>er-delta admission loo<sub>p</sub>: it senses three si<sub>g</sub>nals (claimke<sub>y</sub> identit<sub>y</sub> over content and sco<sub>p</sub>e, embeddin<sub>g</sub> similarit<sub>y</sub>, and an inference verdict), decides via th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>ve-ou</sub>t<sub>come a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ure ga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> C<sub>on</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>, au</sub>th<sub>or</sub>it<sub>y, an</sub>d f<sub>res</sub>h<sub>ness, an</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s</sub> b<sub>y</sub> emittin<sub>g</sub> and a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> a Patch that mutates its own memor<sub>y</sub> (Section 4); the electronic su<sub>pp</sub>lement (A<sub>pp</sub>endix N) tabulates this loo<sub>p</sub> element b<sub>y</sub> element. The loo<sub>p</sub> closes on knowled<sub>g</sub>e admission (the inference verdict drives the sole object that mutates state), but it is not a self-o<sub>p</sub>timizin<sub>g</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>er, as</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s are ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>o</sub>fli<sub>ne an</sub>d h<sub>e</sub>ld fi<sub>xe</sub>d<sub>, w</sub>ith <sub>no run-</sub>ti<sub>me</sub> f<sub>ee</sub>db<sub>ac</sub>k that re-tunes them. Two self-\* properties rest on it: self-healing, the status CRDT reconverging a <sub>p</sub>artitioned, reordered federation to one <sub>p</sub>er-claim status without a coordinator (Section 7.4); and self-protection, authority-gated admission keeping a canonical brain from being silently rewritten by a <sub>p</sub>eer (Section 5.3). Section 5 states how each is bounded: the di<sub>g</sub>est that frees self-healin<sub>g</sub> from rel<sub>y</sub>- in<sub>g</sub> on the semantic-routin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>ath for com<sub>p</sub>lete deliver<sub>y</sub>, and the beni<sub>g</sub>n-fault (non-B<sub>y</sub>zantine) threat model self-<sub>p</sub>rotection ass<sub>u</sub>mes. ## 2.2 The wiki-brain data model MELD synchronizes

A<sub>n</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>vers</sub>i<sub>one</sub>d<sub>,</sub> t<sub>ype</sub>d<sub>-</sub>li<sub>n</sub>k k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>we</sub> <sub>ca</sub>ll it<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>iki b<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>recapp</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l t<sub>ouc</sub>h<sub>es.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> sha<sub>p</sub>e the stores above a<sub>pp</sub>roximate<sub>;</sub> it restates no sin<sub>g</sub>le s<sub>y</sub>stem.

A brain holds nodes (content-addressed, carr<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> an owner identit<sub>y</sub>, an ori<sub>g</sub>in ta<sub>g</sub>, an authorit<sub>y</sub> level, a confidence, an access-control sco<sub>p</sub>e, and a retention state) and first-class links (t<sub>yp</sub>ed, versioned<sub>,</sub> authenticated<sub>,</sub> wei<sub>g</sub>hted relations such as association<sub>,</sub> entit<sub>y</sub> coreference<sub>,</sub> su<sub>p</sub>ersession<sub>,</sub> <sub>p</sub>artial overrule, <sub>g</sub>roundin<sub>g</sub>, and contradiction). A node carries one or more claims: addressable assertions<sub>,</sub> each with a claim ke<sub>y</sub> derived from its canonical content to<sub>g</sub>ether with its discrete sco<sub>p</sub>e<sub>,</sub> a confidence, a validit<sub>y</sub> interval, and a status drawn from a monotone lifec<sub>y</sub>cle (active, de<sub>p</sub>recated, overruled, revoked). Assertions are a<sub>pp</sub>ended, not overwritten: a new version su<sub>p</sub>ersedes an old <sub>one</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>a</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> b<sub>y</sub> <sub>mu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>so</sub> <sub>provenance</sub> <sub>an</sub>d hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>preserve</sub>d<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e</sub> carries a staleness state (a content hash, a fetch time, a freshness bud<sub>g</sub>et, and a latest fla<sub>g</sub>) that <sub>governs w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> it <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ou</sub>ld b<sub>e re-</sub>f<sub>e</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>or a</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> d<sub>ecay.</sub>

Two <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>erties of this model matter for the <sub>p</sub>rotocol. First<sub>,</sub> the natural unit of exchan<sub>g</sub>e is a <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h delta (a version-set of nodes, claims, and links), not a flat document. Second, because links are first class and t<sub>yp</sub>ed, the result of reconcilin<sub>g</sub> two brains can itself be re<sub>p</sub>resented in the <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h (as an association, a coreference, or a contradiction link) rather than colla<sub>p</sub>sed into a sin<sub>g</sub>le overwritten <sub>v</sub>al<sub>u</sub>e. MELD ex<sub>p</sub>loits both.

## 4 Merge semantics

The <sub>p</sub>rocedure returns one of five outcomes for an incomin<sub>g</sub> claim: insert (novel, no matchin<sub>g</sub> candidate), same-enou<sub>g</sub>h, overla<sub>p</sub>s, conflicts, or reject (an admission-<sub>g</sub>ate dro<sub>p</sub>). Once a candidate exists, a <sup>f</sup>our-ver<sup>d</sup>ict pair-a<sup>d</sup>ju<sup>d</sup>ication c<sup>l</sup>assi<sup>fi</sup>er <sup>d</sup>eci<sup>d</sup>es among merge, relate, conflict, an<sup>d</sup> no-relation (no actionable relation), which ma<sub>p</sub>s to insert at the <sub>p</sub>rotocol level (Section 4.2). MELD’s contribution is the decision <sub>p</sub>rocedure that com<sub>p</sub>utes which outcome holds for an incomin<sub>g</sub> claim<sub>,</sub> and the Patch it emits. The <sub>p</sub>rocedure runs <sub>p</sub>er incomin<sub>g</sub> claim � a<sub>g</sub>ainst the claims the receivin<sub>g</sub> brain alread<sub>y</sub> holds<sub>,</sub> delivered b<sub>y</sub> a PUBLISH<sub>,</sub> within a candidate-matched Conce<sub>p</sub>t.

## 4.1 Three signals

Delivery contract. The convergence above asks little of the transport, by design: MELD assumes at-least-once<sub>,</sub> idem<sub>p</sub>otent<sub>,</sub> eventuall<sub>y</sub>-com<sub>p</sub>lete deliver<sub>y</sub> and re<sub>q</sub>uires no orderin<sub>g</sub> or exactl<sub>y</sub>-once <sub>g</sub>uarantee. Re-addin<sub>g</sub> a link id is idem<sub>p</sub>otent (the <sub>g</sub>row-onl<sub>y</sub> set absorbs du<sub>p</sub>licates) and materialization is order-free, so a lo<sub>g</sub>-based fabric’s chea<sub>p</sub> default mode (A<sub>p</sub>ache Kafka’s at-least-once, <sub>p</sub>er-<sub>p</sub>artition orderin<sub>g</sub>) sufices. The <sub>p</sub>remise that does bite is eventual com<sub>p</sub>leteness: a link a brain never receives is missin<sub>g</sub> state<sub>,</sub> so semantic routin<sub>g,</sub> which delivers onl<sub>y</sub> to interested <sub>p</sub>eers at a recall below one (Section 7.5), is backsto<sub>pp</sub>ed b<sub>y</sub> a <sub>p</sub>eriodic anti-entro<sub>py</sub> di<sub>g</sub>est [Demers et al. 1987]. Each brain summarizes its <sub>g</sub>row-onl<sub>y</sub> status-link set as a <sub>p</sub>er-claim, order-inde<sub>p</sub>endent di<sub>g</sub>est (the sorted link ids and a set-hash); two brains exchan<sub>g</sub>e di<sub>g</sub>ests, take the <sub>p</sub>er-claim s<sub>y</sub>mmetric diference, and each <sub>p</sub>ulls and a<sub>pp</sub>lies (idem<sub>p</sub>otent union) the links it lacks, so both reach the same link set (and the same <sub>p</sub>er-claim status) re<sub>g</sub>ardless of routin<sub>g</sub> recall. This makes <sub>p</sub>er-claim status conver<sub>g</sub>ence inde<sub>p</sub>endent of the com<sub>p</sub>leteness of the semantic-routin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>ath: MELD achieves the eventuall<sub>y</sub>-com<sub>p</sub>lete deliver<sub>y</sub> its CRDT needs rather than assumin<sub>g</sub> it of the trans<sub>p</sub>ort. We evaluate the di<sub>g</sub>est under injected routin<sub>g</sub> loss (Section 7.4); self-healin<sub>g</sub> thus holds for <sub>p</sub>artition-<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-reconnec</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> l<sub>ossy rou</sub>ti<sub>ng, prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h li<sub>n</sub>k <sub>surv</sub>i<sub>ves on a</sub>t l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>one reac</sub>h<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e peer</sub> (a link lost b<sub>y</sub> ever<sub>y</sub> brain is the disconnection case, recovered b<sub>y</sub> lo<sub>g</sub> re-consum<sub>p</sub>tion).

## 6.1 Deployment model

The electronic su<sub>pp</sub>lement (A<sub>pp</sub>endix N) illustrates the three-la<sub>y</sub>er stack and the de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>. The de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment frame is a <sub>p</sub>remise rather than a contribution: harnesses (execution) are ke<sub>p</sub>t distinct from a<sub>g</sub>ents (ca<sub>p</sub>abilit<sub>y</sub> and task lo<sub>g</sub>ic), which are ke<sub>p</sub>t distinct from a<sub>g</sub>ent brains (the evolvin<sub>g</sub> knowled<sub>g</sub>e a brain accumulates from both harness and a<sub>g</sub>ent ex<sub>p</sub>erience). Above man<sub>y</sub> agent brains sits a platform-level global brain, created, merged, and tracked on demand under a trust hierarch<sub>y</sub> (Section 5.3). On one harness, the a<sub>g</sub>ent s<sub>p</sub>eaks MCP to tools and MELD to brains (Section 8).

A brain is one versioned, t<sub>yp</sub>ed-link a<sub>g</sub>ent-memor<sub>y</sub> store (Section 2.2): an instance holdin<sub>g</sub> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es</sub> <sub>an</sub>d fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> <sub>as</sub> Z<sub>ep</sub>/G<sub>rap</sub>hiti <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>es</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>o</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>pu</sub>bli<sub>c</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ores</sub> <sub>approx</sub>i<sub>-</sub> mate [Rasmussen et al. 2025; Xu et al. 2025]. Each brain PUBLISHes <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h deltas, content-routed to interested brains<sub>;</sub> the <sub>g</sub>lobal brain is the man<sub>y</sub>-to-one a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation tar<sub>g</sub>et<sub>,</sub> with the mer<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>rocedure <sub>as</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>n</sub>k<sub>.</sub> “Gl<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l” <sub>names</sub> it<sub>s scope ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>:</sub> it i<sub>s an aggrega</sub>ti<sub>on ro</sub>l<sub>e, no</sub>t <sub>a</sub> coordinator. It runs the same mer<sub>g</sub>e-on-receive <sub>p</sub>rocedure as an<sub>y</sub> brain<sub>,</sub> holds no s<sub>p</sub>ecial authorit<sub>y,</sub> and is re<sub>p</sub>roducible at an<sub>y</sub> subscriber<sub>,</sub> so a de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment ma<sub>y</sub> run several. MELD sta<sub>y</sub>s <sub>p</sub>eer-to-<sub>p</sub>eer: the status CRDT conver<sub>g</sub>es the soverei<sub>g</sub>n brains with no coordinator (Section 5.1). ## 7 Evaluation

This section asks four <sub>q</sub>uestions about MELD as an ada<sub>p</sub>tive coherence mechanism<sub>,</sub> in the order that <sub>ma</sub>tt<sub>ers</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>sys</sub>t<sub>em,</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>answere</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t <sub>on</sub> <sub>rea</sub>l <sub>compu</sub>ti<sub>ng-</sub> <sub>co</sub>ntin<sub>uu</sub>m infr<sub>as</sub>tr<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>u</sub>r<sub>e</sub>:

(1) Does MELD ada<sub>p</sub>t safel<sub>y</sub> under conflicting knowledge? The mer<sub>g</sub>e decision <sub>p</sub>rocedure’s classification <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub>, in <sub>p</sub>articular its false-mer<sub>g</sub>e rate, the dan<sub>g</sub>erous error (Section 7.2).

(2) Does it recover from <sub>p</sub>artitions and message loss? The status CRDT’s order-inde<sub>p</sub>endent reconver<sub>g</sub>ence after a real <sub>p</sub>artition heal and under loss<sub>y</sub> routin<sub>g</sub> (Section 7.4).

(3) Does authorit<sub>y</sub> gating <sub>p</sub>revent unsafe state change? A canonical brain is not silentl<sub>y</sub> rewritten b<sub>y</sub> a <sub>p</sub>eer (Sections 5.3 and 7.7).

(4) What ada<sub>p</sub>tation errors remain? The residual false-mer<sub>g</sub>e and contradiction-miss rates, threshold non-transfer across encoders, and the B<sub>y</sub>zantine case left out of sco<sub>p</sub>e (Sections 7.2 and 9.1).

Two further axes establish that this coherence costs little: utilit<sub>y</sub> (whether decentralizin<sub>g</sub> memor<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>reserves what a<sub>g</sub>ents can recall, Section 7.1) and eficienc<sub>y</sub> (s<sub>y</sub>nc latenc<sub>y</sub>, routin<sub>g</sub> selectivit<sub>y</sub>, node-scalin<sub>g</sub>; Sections 7.3, 7.5, and 7.6). The re<sub>p</sub>orted accurac<sub>y</sub> is not, and need not be, cuttin<sub>g</sub>- ed<sub>g</sub>e. The contribution is a new distributed <sub>p</sub>rotocol and mechanism: the <sub>q</sub>uestion is whether it kee<sub>p</sub>s soverei<sub>g</sub>n brains coherent in <sub>p</sub>er-claim status<sub>,</sub> with the semantic <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h structure locall<sub>y</sub> a<sup>d</sup>ju<sup>d</sup>icate<sup>d</sup>, preserving reca<sup>ll</sup> w<sup>h</sup>ere a centra<sup>l</sup>ize<sup>d</sup> store nee<sup>d</sup>s neit<sup>h</sup>er property. T<sup>h</sup>e su<sup>b</sup>strate must su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> a com<sub>p</sub>etence floor: the harness must <sub>g</sub>ive the a<sub>g</sub>ent non-trivial com<sub>p</sub>etence on the task (non-zero, non-saturated accurac<sub>y</sub>), so the measured delta is a real com<sub>p</sub>arison. Two near-zero scores would not distinguish the conditions, and HotpotQA distractor avoids that failure mode. An at-a-<sub>g</sub>lance inventor<sub>y</sub> of all ex<sub>p</sub>eriments (dataset, tier, headline result) is in the electronic su<sub>pp</sub>lement (A<sub>pp</sub>endix I).

Four easil<sub>y</sub>-conflated <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>erties sta<sub>y</sub> distinct: mer<sub>g</sub>e correctness and o<sub>p</sub>erational conver<sub>g</sub>ence (Sections 7.2 and 7.4) are the contribution; retrieval <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> (recall@�, Section 7.1) is the utilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>; answer correc</sub>t<sub>ness s</sub>it<sub>s a</sub>t <sub>merge-vs-cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>par</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> Th<sub>e measura</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>lt<sub>a</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n co</sub>h<sub>erence,</sub> retrieval<sub>,</sub> and stora<sub>g</sub>e. Reasonin<sub>g q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> is out of sco<sub>p</sub>e.

Shared to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>. In ever<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>eriment<sub>,</sub> � a<sub>g</sub>ent <sub>p</sub>rocesses each own a brain store and consume a disjoint (or confi<sub>g</sub>urabl<sub>y</sub> overla<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub>) slice of conversation histories, from which the<sub>y</sub> extract Conce<sub>p</sub>ts<sub>,</sub> Claims<sub>,</sub> and Evidence and PUBLISH <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h deltas<sub>;</sub> the <sub>g</sub>lobal brain is the funnel sink. Testbed: a three-tier com<sub>p</sub>utin<sub>g</sub> continuum. The eval<sub>u</sub>ation r<sub>u</sub>ns across the same com<sub>pu</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> <sub>co</sub>ntin<sub>uu</sub>m MELD i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>g</sub>n<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> k<sub>eep</sub> <sub>co</sub>h<sub>e</sub>r<sub>e</sub>nt<sub>,</sub> <sub>o</sub>n <sub>ope</sub>r<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>a</sub>l Uni<sub>ve</sub>r<sub>s</sub>it<sub>y</sub> <sub>o</sub>f O<sub>u</sub>l<sub>u</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd Finni<sub>s</sub>h national infrastructure. The distributed <sub>p</sub>rotocol ex<sub>p</sub>eriments (s<sub>y</sub>nc latenc<sub>y</sub>, Section 7.3; routin<sub>g</sub>, Section 7.5; <sub>p</sub>artition-heal conver<sub>g</sub>ence, Section 7.4) run on the ed<sub>g</sub>e tier: the Universit<sub>y</sub> of Oulu 5G Test Network (5GTN) [Piri et al. 2016], an o<sub>p</sub>erator-<sub>g</sub>rade 5G test network whose ed<sub>g</sub>e-com<sub>p</sub>utin<sub>g</sub> ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub> we use directl<sub>y</sub>, as four nodes (one broker and the <sub>p</sub>eer brains, $N \in \{ 2 , 3 , 4 \} )$ <sub>,</sub> 4 CPU <sub>co</sub>r<sub>es</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd 8 GB RAM <sub>eac</sub>h<sub>,</sub> D<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>e</sub>r CE <sub>o</sub>n Ub<sub>u</sub>nt<sub>u</sub> 24<sub>.</sub>04<sub>,</sub> <sub>o</sub>n th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sa</sub>m<sub>e</sub> 5GTN <sub>su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>tr<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>use</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>ri<sub>o</sub>r federated <sub>p</sub>ublish/subscribe work [Lovén et al. 2026b]. The headline mer<sub>g</sub>e-<sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>eriment (Section 7.1) runs five o<sub>p</sub>en-wei<sub>g</sub>ht models (three families s<sub>p</sub>annin<sub>g</sub> a ∼ 20× size ran<sub>g</sub>e) on the HPC tier, CSC (the Finnish national su<sub>p</sub>ercom<sub>p</sub>utin<sub>g</sub> centre). The deterministic lo<sub>g</sub>ic ex<sub>p</sub>eriments (mer<sub>g</sub>e classification, the order-inde<sub>p</sub>endence demonstration, and the <sub>g</sub>ate/conflict ablations) run locall<sub>y</sub> on a sin<sub>g</sub>le host. Within the 5GTN ed<sub>g</sub>e, intra-site latenc<sub>y</sub> is under 1 ms (shared LAN); the wide-area latenc<sub>y</sub> re<sub>g</sub>imes are emulated on the ed<sub>g</sub>e nodes via tc qdisc netem (below). ## B Convergence: reject-gate placement

MELD’s wire disci<sub>p</sub>line <sub>g</sub>ates stale and overruled deltas (main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er, Section 5, “Consistenc<sub>y</sub> and trust”). The <sub>g</sub>ate must not become an order-de<sub>p</sub>endent admission filter: were a brain to dro<sub>p</sub> an i<sub>ncom</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m</sub>’<sub>s</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>us a</sub>t <sub>rece</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t ti<sub>me,</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> b<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ns rece</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>or</sub>d<sub>ers</sub> <sub>wou</sub>ld <sub>a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>it dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d di<sub>verge.</sub> MELD th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>it<sub>s</sub> <sub>every</sub> li<sub>n</sub>k i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o</sub> the <sub>g</sub>row-onl<sub>y</sub> set unconditionall<sub>y</sub> and a<sub>pp</sub>lies the <sub>g</sub>ate at materialization-read<sub>,</sub> a <sub>p</sub>ure function of the converged set. On a reject-race scenario (a claim with competing overrule and revoke links plus one stale link, where the stale/overrule <sub>g</sub>ate races the status transition), the materialization-read <sub>g</sub>ate reconver<sub>g</sub>es to a sin<sub>g</sub>le status on all 200/200 orderings (one outcome, revoked); the naive admission-time <sub>g</sub>ate diver<sub>g</sub>es, s<sub>p</sub>littin<sub>g</sub> ≈50/50 between overruled and revoked (101 vs 99 of 200, two distinct outcomes). Read-time <sub>p</sub>lacement <sub>p</sub>reserves stron<sub>g</sub> eventual consistenc<sub>y</sub>.

The main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er (Section 7.8, “Cross-tier de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment and <sub>p</sub>er-delta cost”) re<sub>p</sub>orts a seeded multi-trial <sub>p</sub>artition-heal run directl<sub>y</sub> over the real A<sub>p</sub>ache Kafka WAN<sub>,</sub> u<sub>pg</sub>radin<sub>g</sub> the sin<sub>g</sub>le-session cross-tier existence <sub>p</sub>roof to a reconver<sub>g</sub>ence distribution. To<sub>p</sub>olog<sub>y</sub> and <sub>p</sub>artition model. A la<sub>p</sub>to<sub>p</sub> hub runs the Kafka broker (KRaft mode) and a l<sub>oca</sub>l b<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n; a</sub> CSC HPC b<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> 5GTN <sub>e</sub>d <sub>e</sub> b<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n eac</sub>h <sub>reac</sub>h th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ro</sub>k<sub>er over a rea</sub>l <sub>w</sub>id<sub>e-area</sub> network throu<sub>g</sub>h an ssh -R reverse tunnel. We recor<sup>d</sup>, per tria<sup>l</sup>, <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>par</sub>titi<sub>on</sub> <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d <sub>a</sub> <sub>genu</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> di<sub>vergence</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> <sub>reconverge</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> heal latenc<sub>y</sub> (from link restoration to the ed<sub>g</sub>e readin<sub>g</sub> overruled).

## E Second-substrate recall: LongMemEval

The main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er (Section 7.1) re<sub>p</sub>orts that the headline distributed-versus-centralized recall result <sub>repro</sub>d<sub>uces on a secon</sub>d<sub>,</sub> l<sub>ong-</sub>h<sub>or</sub>i<sub>zon su</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> Thi<sub>s sec</sub>ti<sub>on g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>sweep.</sub>

Method. Lon<sub>g</sub>MemEval (Wu et al., ICLR 2025; arXiv 2410.10813) <sub>p</sub>airs each <sub>q</sub>uestion with a ha<sub>y</sub>stack of chat sessions (distractors <sub>p</sub>lus the <sub>g</sub>old session) in which the evidence turns are fla<sub>gg</sub>ed has\_answer. We ma<sub>p</sub> each <sub>q</sub>uestion to a retrieval task (one context <sub>p</sub>ara<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h <sub>p</sub>er ha<sub>y</sub>stack session, with its turns as units, and each has\_answer turn as a <sub>g</sub>old item) and run the same recall machiner<sub>y</sub> as the Hot<sub>p</sub>otQA headline (main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er, Section 7.1): recall@� of the <sub>g</sub>old evidence turns in the status-live store<sub>,</sub> u<sub>p</sub>stream of an<sub>y</sub> LLM<sub>,</sub> under the centralized<sub>,</sub> distributed-mer<sub>g</sub>e<sub>,</sub> naive-union<sub>,</sub> and exact-dedu<sub>p</sub> conditions. We use 40 <sub>q</sub>uestions (≈ 550 turns each), the all-MiniLM-L6-v2 encoder, overla<sub>p</sub> fractions {0, 0.5}, and $k \in \{ 5 , 1 0 , 2 0 \}$ . ## J Completeness under lossy routing: the anti-entropy digest
